Simple algebras and exact module categories
Kevin Coulembier, Mateusz Stroiński, Tony Zorman

Abstract
. We verify a conjecture of Etingof and Ostrik, stating that an algebra object in a finite tensor category is exact if and only if it is a finite direct product of simple algebras. Towards that end, we introduce an analogue of the Jacobson radical of an algebra object, similar to the Jacobson radical of a finite-dimensional algebra. We give applications of our main results in the context of incompressible finite symmetric tensor categories.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
18D25, 18M05

1.
Introduction

Let
\Bbbk
be an arbitrary field.
Finite tensor categories are abelian
\Bbbk
-linear rigid monoidal categories satisfying finiteness assumptions, generalising fusion categories, see
[
EO1
]
for an overview. They arise naturally from representation theory and logarithmic conformal field theory.

In the theory of module categories, the notion of exact algebras in finite tensor categories is central. It is known that any exact algebra is a product of simple algebras. Here we prove the converse, as conjectured in
[
EO2
, Conjecture B.6]
by Etingof and Ostrik, that every product of simple algebras is exact. In the special case that the finite tensor category is the module category of a finite dimensional Hopf algebra, the statement was known, see
[
EO2
, Proposition B.7]
, since it can be seen to be equivalent to a result of Skryabin in
[
Sk
, Theorem 3.5]
.

Mixed subfunctors

To state our results in more detail, let
\mathcal{C}
be a finite tensor category over
\Bbbk
, with category of projective objects
\mathcal{C}_{p}
. We fix an algebra object
A
in
\mathcal{C}
. We consider the categories
\operatorname{Kl}_{A\otimes\mathcal{C}_{p}}
and
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
of free projective left and right
A
-modules in
\mathcal{C}
. By slight abuse we can say that these categories have the
same
set of objects as
\mathcal{C}_{p}
.
Proving
[
EO2
, Conjecture B.6]
can, via results in
[
EO2
,
St2
]
, be reduced to the following result, see
Theorem 5.7
:

There exists a canonical bijection between the set of ideal objects in
A
and the set of ideals in the category
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
that are stable under the left action of
\mathcal{C}_{p}
.

A seemingly new notion we introduce to establish this bijection is that of a
mixed subfunctor

of the presheaf
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
on
\mathcal{C}_{p}
, see
Subsection 2.2
. These are subfunctors that are simultaneously subfunctors for the interpretation of

\mathcal{C}({-},A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{A{-}}(A\otimes{-},A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}({-}\otimes A,A)

as a presheaf on
\operatorname{Kl}_{A\otimes\mathcal{C}_{p}}
and on
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
, see
Subsection 2.2
for details. By formal manipulations we can see that mixed subfunctors are in bijection with
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideals in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
, see
Section 5
.
We then give two proofs for the fact that mixed subfunctors are in bijection with ideal objects in
A
. One proof uses the idea from
[
EO2
]
to realise a finite tensor category via a pseudo-Hopf algebra. The other takes a more categorical approach, using the monoidal structure on the presheaf category
[\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}]
on
\mathcal{C}
given by
Day convolution
, and establishing a correspondence between mixed subfunctors and representable ideal objects in the algebra object
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
, which in turn correspond to ideals in
A
.

Using mixed subfunctors as a bridge between ideals in
A
and
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideals in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
, we connect the latter kind of ideals with exactness of
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
as a
\mathcal{C}
-module category, by expanding on
[
MM
, Lemma 12]
, and its generalisations
[
KMMZ
, Theorem 11]
,
[
St2
, Theorem 4.17]
. Namely, we find that the minimal projective presentation of an object of the form
Q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}M
, for
M\in\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
and
Q\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
, generates a
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
. Minimality implies that this generator, and hence also the generated ideal, are contained in the Jacobson radical of
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
. It also implies that this generator is zero if and only if
Q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}M
is projective.
This allows us to develop a theory of Jacobson radicals for algebra objects in finite tensor categories analogous to that for finite-dimensional algebras.

In particular, to an algebra object
A
we associate an ideal
\mathtt{Rad}^{\mathcal{C}}(A)
in
A
, which, under
Theorem 5.7
corresponds to the greatest nilpotent
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
, and hence
\mathtt{Rad}^{\mathcal{C}}(A)
itself is the greatest nilpotent ideal object in
A
. We can now formulate our main result:

Theorem

(
Theorem 7.1
)
.

Let
A
be an algebra object in a finite tensor category
\mathcal{C}
. The following are equivalent:

(i)

A
is exact.

(ii)

\mathtt{Rad}^{\mathcal{C}}(A)=0
.

(iii)

A
has no non-zero nilpotent ideals.

(iv)

A
is a finite direct product of simple algebras.

This is again analogous with the theory of finite-dimensional algebras, since
A
is exact if and only if every
M\in\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
is
\mathcal{C}
-projective
, i.e. its internal hom functor
\lfloor M,{-}\rfloor
is exact, so exactness of
A
is analogous, and, as we show, equivalent, to semisimplicity.
Finally, in
Theorem 8.8
, we show that the category of
A/\mathtt{Rad}^{\mathcal{C}}(A)
-modules, viewed as a full subcategory of
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
, consists precisely of the subquotients of objects of the form
Q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}L
, for
Q\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
and
L
a semisimple object of
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
. In order to do this, we give a description of the submodule
\mathtt{Rad}^{\mathcal{C}}(M)=M\mathtt{Rad}^{\mathcal{C}}(A)
of
M
in
8.2
.

In the presence of a fibre functor, where
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
is the category of modules over an
H
-module algebra, for a finite-dimensional Hopf algebra
H
, the properties of
\mathtt{Rad}^{\mathcal{C}}(A)
, given by the largest
H
-stable ideal in the radical of
A
, were studied previously in
[
LMS
]
, and, in a more general Hopf-theoretic setting, in
[
Fi
]
.

Motivation and applications

The base example of a finite tensor category is the category
\mathbf{vecfd}
of finite dimensional vector spaces. In
\mathbf{vecfd}
every simple algebra is a Frobenius algebra. It is known that this statement does not extend to general finite tensor categories, see
[
Sh
, Example 5.24]
. In
[
Sh
]
, Shimizu introduced the notion of a quasi-Frobenius algebra (which are algebras Morita equivalent to Frobenius algebras), and conjectured that all simple algebras are quasi-Frobenius, see
[
Sh
, Conjecture 5.25]
. As noted
loc cit.
the conjecture is equivalent to
[
EO2
, Conjecture B.6]
, so we also prove Shimizu’s conjecture.

If
\mathcal{C}
is braided and
A
is an indecomposable commutative algebra in
A
, then the category of
A
-modules in
\mathcal{C}
is again a (finite) tensor category if and only if
A
is exact. It thus follows from the result in this paper that any simple commutative algebra gives rise to another tensor category. This is of interest for instance in modular and symmetric cases:

In the theory of vertex operator algebras, for a vertex operator algebra extension
V\subset W
, it is so that
W
becomes a commutative algebra in the braided monoidal representation category of
V
(under suitable assumptions) such that the representation category of
W
is obtained as the category of (local) modules over this algebra. Our result can thus be used to get broader results on when representation categories of vertex operator algebras are rigid. For instance, see
[
SY
, Theorem 5.21]
and
[
CMSY
, Theorem 2.24]
for results on these questions that can now be extended.

For symmetric (not necessarily finite) tensor categories, it is an interesting question in the ongoing classification of incompressible categories whether simple commutative (ind-)algebras are exact, see
[
CEO
, Question 6.1.4]
. We thus answer this question in the affirmative for finite symmetric tensor categories. We give some more detailed applications of our results to finite symmetric tensor categories in
Appendix B
.

Structure

The remainder of the paper is organised as follows. In
Section 2
, we recall some preliminaries and introduce notation for finite tensor categories, their categories of projective objects, their presheaf categories, module categories and ideals therein. Further, we introduce the notion of a mixed subfunctor (
Subsection 2.2
) and establish the notation for all the kinds of ideals we will consider. In
Section 3
, we establish a bijection (
3.2
) between ideals in an algebra object
A
in a finite tensor category
\mathcal{C}
, and mixed subfunctors of
{\mathcal{C}({-},A)}_{|\mathcal{C}_{p}}
, using a realisation of
\mathcal{C}
via a pseudo-Hopf algebra. We establish the same bijection using Day convolution and representable presheaves in
Section 4
. We then establish a bijection between mixed subfunctors of
{\mathcal{C}({-},A)}_{|\mathcal{C}_{p}}
and
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideals in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
in
Section 5
, resulting in a bijection between the latter kind of ideals, and ideals in
A
,
Theorem 5.7
. In
Section 6
, we define and study products of various kinds of ideals, introduce the
\mathcal{C}
-module radical of an algebra object (
Subsection 6.2
), and show that
A
is exact if and only if
\mathtt{Rad}^{\mathcal{C}}(A)=0
(
Theorem 6.9
), which implies
[
EO2
, Conjecture B.6]
.
In
Section 7
, we first give a module-theoretic proof of the fact that an exact algebra is a finite direct product of simple algebras (
7.4
). We then use the results of preceding sections to state our main result,
Theorem 7.1
, and give a short proof thereof.
In
Section 8
, we define the radical of a module object (
Section 8
), using which we characterise
A/\mathtt{Rad}^{\mathcal{C}}(A)
-modules (
Theorem 8.8
).

Appendix A
explains how our results specialize to Skryabin’s result,
[
Sk
, Theorem 3.5]
, in the presence of a fibre functor on
\mathcal{C}
.
The paper concludes with
Appendix B
, which contains the applications to finite symmetric tensor categories.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Finite tensor categories

Let
\Bbbk
be a field. We refer to
[
EGNO
]
for the standard notions of (finite) tensor categories over
\Bbbk
. However, contrary to
[
EGNO
]
we do not assume that the base field
\Bbbk
is algebraically closed. A
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal category
(\mathcal{C},\otimes,\mathbb{1})
(with
\Bbbk
-bilinear tensor product) is a finite tensor category if

(1)

(\mathcal{C},\otimes,\mathbb{1})
is rigid, meaning every
X\in\mathcal{C}
has a left dual
{\vphantom{\!X}}{}^{\vee}\!X
and a right dual
{X}^{\vee}
(denoted by
X^{\ast}
and
{}^{\ast}X
respectively in
[
EGNO
]
).
Given an object
V\in\mathcal{C}
, we denote the

evaluation
and
coevaluation
(sometimes referred to as
counit
and
unit
) morphisms as follows:

\mathrm{ev}_{V}\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu{\vphantom{\!V}}^{\vee}\!V\otimes V\longrightarrow\mathbb{1},\;\mathrm{coev}_{V}\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbb{1}\longrightarrow V\otimes{\vphantom{\!V}}^{\vee}\!V

(2)

\mathcal{C}
is equivalent to the category of finite dimensional modules of a finite dimensional associative
\Bbbk
-algebra, meaning that
\mathcal{C}
has enough projective objects, finite dimensional morphism spaces, objects of finite length and only finitely many simple objects up to isomorphism.

(3)

\Bbbk\longrightarrow\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{1})
is an isomorphism.

Useful properties that follow from these assumptions is that the tensor product is exact and
\mathbb{1}
is a simple object. As a consequence of the latter, for any non-zero object
V\in\mathcal{C}
, the coevaluation morphism
\mathrm{coev}_{{V}}
is mono and the evaluation morphism
\mathrm{ev}_{{V}}
is epic.

Throughout the paper, let
\mathcal{C}
be a finite tensor category over
\Bbbk
, and let
\mathcal{C}_{p}
denote the full subcategory of projective objects in
\mathcal{C}
. Further, throughout we let

A=(A,\mu,\eta)
be an algebra object in
\mathcal{C}
, let
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
denote the category of right
A
-module objects, and let
\operatorname{proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
denote the full subcategory of projective objects in
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
. For
M,N\in\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
, we write
\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(M,N)
for
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)(M,N)
and similarly the notation
\operatorname{Hom}_{A{-}}
refers to morphisms of left
A
-modules.

Recall that
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
is a
\mathcal{C}
-module category, where, for
M\in\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
and
V\in\mathcal{C}
, the action of
V
on
M
is defined as the following
A
-module:

(2.1)

(V\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}M,\nabla_{V\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}M})=(V\otimes M,V\otimes\nabla_{M}).

We use the notation
V\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}M
, rather than the common alternative
V\otimes M
, both to make our choice of
A
-action unambiguous, but also to indicate that many of our definitions and results can be translated into the setting of abstract
\mathcal{C}
-module categories. For a left
A
-module
N
, we find a similarly defined left
A
-module
N\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}V
.

Definition 2.0
.

Let
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
denote the full subcategory of
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
whose objects are those of the form
P\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A
for
P\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
. The notation indicates that this is the Kleisli category for the monad
{-}\otimes A
on
\mathcal{C}_{p}
, viewed as a subcategory of
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
, which itself is the Eilenberg–Moore category for
{-}\otimes A
viewed as a monad on
\mathcal{C}
. In particular, being a Kleisli category, the object set of
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
canonically identifies with the object set of
\mathcal{C}_{p}
, on which the monad
{-}\otimes A
operates.

In
Subsection 2.1
below, we only consider the cases
\mathcal{M}=\operatorname{proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
and
\mathcal{M}=\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
.

Definition 2.0
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a
\mathcal{C}
-module subcategory of
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
. A
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal

\mathcal{J}
in
\mathcal{M}
consists of a collection of subspaces

\mathcal{J}(M,N)\subseteq\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(M,N)

such that for
f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(M,M^{\prime})
and
g\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(M^{\prime},M^{\prime\prime})
, we have
g\circ f\in\mathcal{J}(M,M^{\prime\prime})
if
f\in\mathcal{J}(M,M^{\prime})
or
g\in\mathcal{J}(M^{\prime},M^{\prime\prime})
, and
f\in\mathcal{J}(M,M^{\prime})
implies
Q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}f\in\mathcal{J}(Q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}M,Q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}M^{\prime})
, for
Q\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
.

Notation 2.0
.

In the sequel, we will use four different kinds of structure, between which we will establish a chain of bijections. In order to maintain clear distinction between these, we record the notation we will use for each here:

(N1)

We write the
mixed subfunctors
of
\mathcal{C}({-},A)\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathcal{C}_{p}^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Vec}
(see
Subsection 2.2
) in a monospaced font:
\mathtt{I,J}
, etc.

(N2)

We denote the
ideals in the algebra
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
(see
2.5
), in particular also the representable ideals in
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
, by upright Roman letters:
\EuRoman{I,J}
, etc.

(N3)

We denote the
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideals in
\operatorname{proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
, as well as ideals in categories more generally, by calligraphic letters:
\mathcal{I,J}
, etc.

(N4)

As an exception to
(N3)
, we write the
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideals in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
(see
Subsection 2.1
) in Fraktur:
\mathfrak{I,J}
, etc.
For
P,P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
, we write
\mathfrak{J}(P,Q)
for
\mathfrak{J}(P\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A,Q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A)
.

Lemma 2.1
.

Any object of
\,\operatorname{proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
is a direct summand of an object in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
. Thus,
\operatorname{proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
is the idempotent closure of
\,\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
in
\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
.

Notation 2.1
.

We use symbols
P,Q,P^{\prime},Q^{\prime}
and the like to denote the objects of
\mathcal{C}_{p}
. We use symbols
R,S,R^{\prime},S^{\prime}
and the like to denote the objects of
\operatorname{proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
. When associating a projective object to a not necessarily projective object, we use the latter as a superscript for the former. Thus, for example, using the standard subscript notation for projective resolutions, we may write the projective resolution of an object
V\in\mathcal{C}
as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cdots}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{2}^{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{1}^{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{0}^{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}^{V}}

The following is a well-known property of idempotent closures, see e.g.
[
St1
, Corollary 10.15]
.

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
\mathcal{J}
be a
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal in
\operatorname{proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
. Then
\mathcal{J}_{|\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}}
defines a
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
. Further, a
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal
\mathfrak{J}
in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
admits a unique extension
\mathfrak{J}^{\mathsf{c}}
to a
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal in
\operatorname{proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
, establishing a bijection between
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideals in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
and in
\operatorname{proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
.

Let
\mathbf{Vec}
denote the category of (not necessarily finite-dimensional) vector spaces over
\Bbbk
, and let
\mathbf{vecfd}
denote its full subcategory of finite-dimensional vector spaces.
Recall that the category
[\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}]
of
\Bbbk
-linear presheaves on
\mathcal{C}
is monoidal under so-called
Day convolution
. For
\Phi,\Psi\in[\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}]
, their tensor product is given by

(\Phi\oast\Psi)(X)=\int^{U,V\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}(X,U\otimes V)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\Phi(U)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\Psi(V).

In particular, the Yoneda embedding
\text{\begin{CJK}{min}よ\end{CJK}}\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow[\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}]
is a fully faithful, left exact, strong monoidal
\Bbbk
-linear functor. We can characterise its image as follows:

Lemma 2.3
.

A presheaf
\Phi\in[\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}]
is representable if and only if it is left exact and
\Phi(V)
is finite-dimensional for all
V\in\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

The Ind-completion
\mathbf{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
is a locally finitely presentable category, whose category of compact objects is precisely
\mathcal{C}
.
Since a presheaf
\Phi\in[\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}]
is left exact if and only if its category of elements is filtered,
in that case it is of the form
\Phi\cong\mathrm{fcolim}_{i}\mathcal{C}({-},V_{i})
.
Thus, the functor

\mathbf{Ind}(\mathcal{C})({-},\Phi)\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbf{Ind}(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow{\vphantom{\mathbf{Vec}}}\mathbf{Vec}{}
factors through the Yoneda embedding, and there is an isomorphism
\mathbf{Ind}(\mathcal{C})({-},\Phi)|_{\text{\begin{CJK}{min}よ\end{CJK}}(\mathcal{C})}\cong\Phi
.

If
W\in\mathcal{C}
then
\mathbf{Ind}(\mathcal{C})(V,W)\cong\mathcal{C}(V,W)
is finite-dimensional. On the other hand, if
\,\mathcal{C}(P,W)
is finite-dimensional for all
P\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
then
W
is of finite length, and so
W\in\mathcal{C}
.
∎

Since
よ
is a monoidal functor, it sends algebra and (bi)module objects in
\mathcal{C}
to algebra and (bi)module objects in
[\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}]
endowed with Day convolution. In particular, for an algebra object
A
in
\mathcal{C}
, the multiplication on
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
is given by the map

(2.2)

\mu_{\mathcal{C}({-},A)}\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\int^{V,W\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}({-},V\otimes W)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}(V,A)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}(W,A)\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}({-},A)

corresponding to the collection

(2.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(X,V\otimes W)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}(V,A)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}(W,A)\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}(X,A),

\displaystyle\varphi\otimes g\otimes h\longmapsto\mu_{A}\circ(g\otimes h)\circ\varphi

dinatural in
V,W
, under the universal property of the coend.
Henceforth,
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
will always be considered as an algebra object in the presheaf category
[\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}]
.

Corollary 2.4
.

Let
I
be an ideal in
A
, then the presheaf
\mathcal{C}({-},I)
is an ideal in the algebra
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
.

Corollary 2.5
.

A subpresheaf
\EuRoman{I}
of
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
is an ideal in
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
if for all
U,V,W\in\mathcal{C}
, all
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}(U,V\otimes W)
and morphisms
f\in\mathcal{C}(V,A),g\in\mathcal{C}(W,A)
, we have
\mu_{A}\circ(f\otimes g)\circ\varphi\in\EuRoman{I}(U)
provided that either
f\in\EuRoman{I}(V)
or
g\in\EuRoman{I}(W)
.

Proof.

Being an ideal in the algebra object
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
implies that
\EuRoman{I}
is a left
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
-submodule of
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
, i.e. that for every
U\in\mathcal{C}
, the image of the map

(2.4)

\displaystyle\int^{V,W\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}(U,V\otimes W)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}(V,A)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\EuRoman{I}(W)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\int^{V,W\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}(U,V\otimes W)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}(V,A)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}(W,A)\xrightarrow{{(\mu_{\mathcal{C}({-},A)})}_{U}}\mathcal{C}(U,A)

is contained in
\EuRoman{I}(U)
. Secondly,
\EuRoman{I}
is a right
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
-submodule of
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
, so that also the image of the map

(2.5)

\displaystyle\int^{V,W\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}(U,V\otimes W)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\EuRoman{I}(V)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}(W,A)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\int^{V,W\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{C}(U,V\otimes W)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}(V,A)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}(W,A)\xrightarrow{{(\mu_{\mathcal{C}({-},A)})}_{U}}\mathcal{C}(U,A)

is contained in
\EuRoman{I}(U)
. Using the description of the multiplication on
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
following
Equation 2.2
, we can reformulate these conditions as stating that

(2.6)

\mu_{A}\circ(g\otimes h)\circ\varphi\in\EuRoman{I}(U),

for any
\varphi\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muU\longrightarrow V\otimes W,g\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muV\longrightarrow A
and
h\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muW\longrightarrow A
, whenever
g
or
h
lie in
\EuRoman{I}(V)
or
\EuRoman{I}(W)
, respectively.
∎

We will denote the restriction of a presheaf
\Phi
on a category
\mathcal{A}
to a subcategory
\mathcal{B}
of
\mathcal{A}
by
\Phi_{|\mathcal{B}}
. Our cases of interest will be
{\mathcal{C}({-},A)}_{|\mathcal{C}_{p}}
in
Subsection 2.2
below, and
{\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}({-},M)}_{|\operatorname{proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)}
, for
M\in\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
, in
Section 8
.

2.2.
Mixed subfunctors

Let
M\in\mathcal{C}
, let
N\in\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
and let
N^{\prime}
be a left
A
-module in
\mathcal{C}
. Given
f\in\mathcal{C}(M,N)
and
h\in\mathcal{C}(M,N^{\prime})
, let
f^{A}=\nabla_{N}\circ(f\otimes A)\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(M\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A,N)
and we define
{}^{A}h\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A{-}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}M,N^{\prime})
similarly.

Given
g\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(M\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A,N)
, let
g^{1}=g\circ(M\otimes\eta)\in\mathcal{C}(M,N)
and given
h^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A{-}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}M,N^{\prime})
, define
{}^{1}h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}(M,N^{\prime})
similarly.
In particular
{(f^{A})}^{1}=f
and
{(g^{1})}^{A}=g
and similarly for the left module structures, yielding familiar isomorphisms

(2.7)

\mathcal{C}(M,N)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(M\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A,N)\text{ and }\mathcal{C}(M,N^{\prime})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{A{-}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}M,N^{\prime}).

Definition 2.5
.

A
mixed subfunctor of
\,{\mathcal{C}({-},A)}_{|\mathcal{C}_{p}}
consists of, for
P\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
, a collection of subspaces
\mathtt{J}(P)\subseteq\mathcal{C}(P,A)
, that satisfy the following conditions:

(M1)

\mathtt{J}
is a subpresheaf of
{\mathcal{C}({-},A)}_{|\mathcal{C}_{p}}
: for any
P,P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
and any
g\in\mathtt{J}(P)
and
f\in\mathcal{C}(P^{\prime},P)
, the morphism
g\circ f\in\mathcal{C}(P^{\prime},A)
lies in
\mathtt{J}(P^{\prime})
.

(M2)

The collections
\{\,g^{A}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(P\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A,A)\mid g\in\mathtt{J}(P)\,\}
define a subpresheaf of the presheaf
{\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}({-},A)}_{|\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}}
: for
g\in\mathtt{J}(P)
and
f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(Q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A,P\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A)
, the morphism
g^{A}\circ f
is of the form
h^{A}
for
h\in\mathtt{J}(Q)
. Equivalently,
{(g^{A}\circ f)}^{1}\in\mathtt{J}(Q)
.

(M3)

The collections
\{\,{}^{A}g\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A{-}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}P,A)\mid g\in\mathtt{J}(P)\,\}
define a subpresheaf of the presheaf
{\operatorname{Hom}_{A{-}}({-},A)}_{|\operatorname{Kl}_{A\otimes\mathcal{C}_{p}}}
: for
g\in\mathtt{J}(P)
and
f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A{-}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}Q,A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}P)
, the morphism
{}^{A}g\circ f
is of the form
{}^{A}h
for
h\in\mathtt{J}(Q)
. Equivalently,
{}^{1}({}^{A}g\circ f)\in\mathtt{J}(Q)
.

Note that that condition
(M1)
is implied by either
(M2)
or
(M3)
.

3.
Algebra ideals and mixed subfunctors, first approach

Fix a projective generator
\mathfrak{P}
of
\mathcal{C}
.
Since the category of presheaves on
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
is equivalent to the category of right modules over
\operatorname{End}_{{-}A}(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A)
, and similarly for right
\operatorname{End}_{A{-}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}\mathfrak{P})
-modules, we have an immediate bijection between mixed subfunctors and subspaces of

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathfrak{P},A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A,A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{A{-}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}\mathfrak{P},A)

that are submodules for both of these algebras. We will derive explicit descriptions of the actions of both algebras in
Subsection 3.1
and then use them to link mixed subfunctors to algebra ideals.

3.1.
The set-up

3.1.1.
The pseudo-Hopf algebra

Consider the faithful exact functor

\omega\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{C}(\mathfrak{P},-)\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathbf{vecfd}.

We thus obtain an equivalence between
\mathcal{C}
and the category of finite dimensional (over
\Bbbk
) left
H
-modules, for

H\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{End}(\omega)={\operatorname{End}(\mathfrak{P})}^{\mathrm{op}}.

Under this equivalence, the projective generator
\mathfrak{P}
of
\mathcal{C}
is sent to the left regular
H
-module and
\mathbb{1}
is sent to some simple
H
-module
L
. For the rest of this section, we will
redefine

\mathcal{C}
as the category of
H
-modules, justifying notation as
\mathbb{1}=L
. Furthermore,
\omega
thus becomes the functor forgetting the
H
-action, which we often leave out of notation.

As explained in
[
EO2
, § 4]
, the tensor category structure on
\mathcal{C}
equips
H
with the structure of a ‘pseudo-Hopf algebra’. We do not need to review all features, but the tensor product on
\mathcal{C}
is encoded in an
(H,H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H)
-bimodule
T
, so that

X\otimes Y\;=\;T\otimes_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(X\otimes_{\Bbbk}Y)

for objects (
H
-modules)
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}
. Here, and for the rest of this section, in order to avoid confusion, the undecorated symbols
\otimes,\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}
and
\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}

refers to the tensor product inside
\mathcal{C}
and the actions of
\mathcal{C}
on left and right
A
-modules, while for tensor products over
\Bbbk
-algebras
R
we will always use
\otimes_{R}
.
We also have natural isomorphisms

(3.1)

T\otimes_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(L\otimes_{\Bbbk}-)\;\cong\;\mathrm{id}\;\cong\;T\otimes_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(-\otimes_{\Bbbk}L),

where
\mathrm{id}
is the identity functor on
\mathcal{C}
.

3.1.2.
Action maps

Regarding
A
as an object in
\mathcal{C}
(
i.e.
an
H
-module) gives an action map

\rho\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muH\otimes_{\Bbbk}A\longrightarrow A,

which is again a morphism of
H
-modules (for
A
regarded as a vector space in the source).
We can also write the multiplication map as

\mu\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muT\otimes_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(A\otimes_{\Bbbk}A)\longrightarrow A,

and the unit as
\eta\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muL\longrightarrow A
.

We have isomorphisms of vector spaces

\operatorname{End}_{A{-}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}\mathfrak{P})\cong\mathcal{C}(\mathfrak{P},A\otimes\mathfrak{P})=\omega(A\otimes\mathfrak{P})\cong T\otimes_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(A\otimes_{\Bbbk}H)\cong T\otimes_{H{-}}A.

Here, we write subscripts like
H{-}
to indicate that we take the tensor product over
H\otimes_{\Bbbk}\Bbbk\subset H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H
.
This thus equips
T\otimes_{H{-}}A
with the structure of a
\Bbbk
-algebra, which we will not need to describe explicitly. By construction, it boasts a right module structure on

\operatorname{Hom}_{A{-}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}\mathfrak{P},A)\cong\mathcal{C}(\mathfrak{P},A)=\omega(A).

It will be more convenient to write the algebra on the left in the corresponding action map,
i.e.
as

(T\otimes_{H{-}}A)\otimes_{\Bbbk}\omega(A)\;\longrightarrow\;\omega(A).

By direct verification, up to composition with an isomorphism, this action map is

(3.2)

T\otimes_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(A\otimes_{\Bbbk}(H\otimes_{\Bbbk}\omega(A)))\xrightarrow{T_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(A\otimes_{\Bbbk}\rho)}T\otimes_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(A\otimes_{\Bbbk}A)\xrightarrow{\mu}\omega(A).

We included all
\omega
’s in the formula that make sense, to stress that
H
does not act on the second copy of
A
in the source, and to indicate that
\omega(A)
is the module on which we act.

Similarly, the action of

\operatorname{End}_{{-}A}(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A)\;\cong\;T\otimes_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(H\otimes_{\Bbbk}A)

on
\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(\mathfrak{P}\otimes A,A)\cong\omega(A)

is captured by the action map

(3.3)

T\otimes_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}((H\otimes_{\Bbbk}\omega(A))\otimes_{\Bbbk}A)\xrightarrow{T_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(\rho\otimes_{\Bbbk}A)}T\otimes_{H\otimes_{\Bbbk}H}(A\otimes_{\Bbbk}A)\xrightarrow{\mu}\omega(A).

3.2.
The bijection

Proposition 3.1
.

A subspace

V\subset\omega(A)=\mathcal{C}(\mathfrak{P},A)

corresponds to a two-sided ideal in the algebra
A\in\mathcal{C}
if and only if it is a submodule for both the
\operatorname{End}_{{-}A}(\mathfrak{P}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A)
-module and the
\operatorname{End}_{A{-}}(A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleleft$}}}}}\mathfrak{P})
-module structure on
\mathcal{C}(\mathfrak{P},A)
.

Proof.

Assume first that
V\subset\omega(A)
represents a two-sided ideal in
A
. That means that it is an
H
-submodule, or in other words that
\rho
restricts to

H\otimes_{\Bbbk}V\longrightarrow V

and similarly that
\mu
restricts to

T\otimes_{H\otimes H}(A\otimes_{\Bbbk}V)\longrightarrow V\quad\mbox{and}\quad T\otimes_{H\otimes H}(V\otimes_{\Bbbk}A)\longrightarrow V.

Hence
V\subset\omega(A)
is stable under actions (
3.2
) and (
3.3
), making it a submodule for both actions.

Conversely, assume that a subspace
V\subset\omega(A)
is stable for both action maps (
3.2
) and (
3.3
). Using either action, the unit of
A
and (
3.1
), shows that
V
is an
H
-submodule. Since
\rho
is an epimorphism of
H
-modules and the tensor product is exact, we find a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\otimes_{H\otimes H}(A\otimes_{\Bbbk}(H\otimes_{\Bbbk}V))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\otimes_{H\otimes H}(A\otimes_{\Bbbk}V)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\omega(A)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T\otimes_{H\otimes H}(A\otimes\rho)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

where the diagonal is the action (
3.2
) restricted to
V\subset\omega(A)
. By assumption this action takes values in
V\subset\omega(A)
. Since the horizontal arrow is surjective, also
\mu
must take values in
V
implying that
V
is a left ideal. Using (
3.3
) shows that
V
is a right ideal too.
∎

Passing throught the equivalence between mixed subfunctors and ‘double’ submodules of
\mathcal{C}(\mathfrak{P},A)
then yields the following consequence of
3.1
.

Corollary 3.2
.

The assignment that sends an ideal
I<A
to the presheaf

\mathcal{C}_{p}^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{vecfd},\quad P\longmapsto\mathcal{C}(P,I),

yields a bijection between ideals in
A
and mixed subfunctors of
\,\mathcal{C}({-},A)|_{\mathcal{C}_{p}}
.

4.
Algebra ideals and mixed subfunctors, second approach

4.1.
Reduction of presheaves to projective objects

Lemma 4.1
.

A left exact presheaf
\Phi\in[\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}]
is uniquely determined by its restriction to the subcategory of projective objects in
\mathcal{C}
, i.e. by

\mathcal{C}_{p}^{\operatorname{op}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}}\xrightarrow{\Phi}\mathbf{Vec}_{\Bbbk}.

Furthermore, this restriction yields an equivalence
\mathbf{Lex}(\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}_{\Bbbk})\simeq\mathbf{Fun}_{\Bbbk}(\mathcal{C}_{p}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{Vec}_{\Bbbk})
, which also restricts to an equivalence
\mathbf{Lex}(\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{vecfd}_{\Bbbk})\simeq\mathbf{Fun}_{\Bbbk}(\mathcal{C}_{p}^{\operatorname{op}},\mathbf{vecfd}_{\Bbbk})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{C}
has enough projectives,
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}}
has enough injectives, which are given precisely by
\mathcal{C}_{p}^{\operatorname{op}}
. The claim follows by a standard result about abelian categories with enough injectives.
∎

Before proving the result of this section, we need to recall a slight variant of a result of
[
KL
]
:

Proposition 4.2

(
[
KL
, Proposition 5.1.7]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an abelian
\Bbbk
-linear category and
\mathcal{P}
a collection of objects in
\mathcal{C}
such that for any
X\in\mathcal{C}
, there is an epimorphism
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}P_{i}\twoheadrightarrow X
, for some
n
and
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
. Let
\Phi\!\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Vec}
be a functor such that
\Phi(-,Y)
is left exact and
\Phi(X,-)
is exact, for all
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}
. The morphism

\int^{P\in\mathcal{P}}\Phi(P,P)\longrightarrow\int^{X\in\mathcal{C}}\Phi(X,X)

induced by the inclusion
\left\{\Phi(P,P)\right\}_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\subseteq\left\{\Phi(X,X)\right\}_{X\in\mathcal{C}}
, is an isomorphism.

Proof.

While the statement of
[
KL
, Proposition 5.1.7]
assumes also
\Phi(-,Y)
to be exact, the assumption about the right exactness of
\Phi(-,Y)
is not used in the proof given therein.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

In our application of
4.2
in the proof of
4.3
below, we could instead also directly invoke
[
KL
, Remark 5.1.10]
.

Proposition 4.3
.

A representable ideal
\EuRoman{I}
in
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
is determined by the collection of subspaces
\EuRoman{I}(P)\subseteq\mathcal{C}(P,A)
, for
P\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
.

Further, a collection
\{\,\EuRoman{I}(P)\subseteq\mathcal{C}(P,A)\mid P\in\mathcal{C}_{p}\,\}
is the restriction of a representable ideal in
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
if and only if the following conditions are satisfied:

(D1)

\EuRoman{I}
is a subpresheaf of
\,{\mathcal{C}({-},A)}_{|\mathcal{C}_{p}}
: for any
P,P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
and any
g\in\EuRoman{I}(P)
and
f\in\mathcal{C}(P^{\prime},P)
, the morphism
g\circ f\in\mathcal{C}(P^{\prime},A)
lies in
\EuRoman{I}(P^{\prime})
.

(D2)

\EuRoman{I}
is the restriction of a left
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
-submodule object of
\,\mathcal{C}({-},A)
: for any
Q,P,P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
, any
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}(Q,P\otimes P^{\prime})
, any
g\in\mathcal{C}(P,A)
and
h\in\EuRoman{I}(P^{\prime})
, the morphism
\mu_{A}\circ(g\otimes h)\circ\varphi
lies in
\EuRoman{I}(Q)
.

(D3)

\EuRoman{I}
is the restriction a right
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
-submodule object of
\,\mathcal{C}({-},A)
: for any
Q,P,P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
, any
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}(Q,P\otimes P^{\prime})
, any
g\in\EuRoman{I}(P)
and
h\in\mathcal{C}(P^{\prime},A)
, the morphism
\mu_{A}\circ(g\otimes h)\circ\varphi
lies in
\EuRoman{I}(Q)
.

Proof.

By definition, an ideal
\mathrm{I}
in
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
consists of subspaces
\mathrm{I}(V)\subseteq\mathcal{C}(V,A)
which give a subpresheaf, thus in particular the collection
\left\{\mathcal{C}(P,A)\right\}_{P\in\mathcal{C}_{p}}
satisfies condition
(D1)
. Further, this collection determines
\mathrm{I}
, by
4.1
.
Since representable presheaves are closed under Day convolution, we find that
\mathrm{I}\oast\mathcal{C}({-},A)
and
\mathcal{C}({-},A)\oast\mathrm{I}
are both left exact, so we may assume
U
to be projective in
Equation 2.6
. This implies that the profunctors whose coends we consider in
Equation 2.4
and
Equation 2.5
above are left exact in the contravariant variable and exact in the covariant variables, so by
4.2
, we may also assume
V,W
to be projective.
∎

4.2.
Mixed subfunctors and representable presheaf ideals

Lemma 4.4
.

A collection of subspaces
\EuRoman{J}(P)\subseteq\mathcal{C}(P,A)
, for
P\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
, determining a representable ideal in
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
is a mixed subfunctor.

\EuRoman{J}

{\mathcal{C}({-},A)}_{|\mathcal{C}_{p}}

to morphisms

, and
\varphi

and

.

To verify condition

∎

5.
Mixed subfunctors and
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideals

5.1.
From
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideals to mixed subfunctors

Recall that
\mathcal{C}_{p}
is closed under taking duals.
Since the bijection we aim to establish is invariant under monoidal equivalence, we may assume
\mathcal{C}
to be strict, in particular that
X\otimes\mathbb{1}=X=\mathbb{1}\otimes X
for
X\in\mathcal{C}
, and
(P\otimes Q)\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}M=P\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}(Q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}M)
for
M\in\operatorname{mod}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
.

Definition 5.0
.

Let
\mathfrak{J}
be a
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
. Let
Q\xrightarrow{q}\mathbb{1}
be an epimorphism in
\mathcal{C}
, for
Q\in\mathcal{C}_{p}
.
We define a mixed subfunctor
\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})
of
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
by

(5.1)

\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P)=\left\{(q\otimes A)\circ g^{1}\;|\;g\in\mathfrak{J}(P,Q)\subseteq\operatorname{Hom}_{{-}A}(P\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A,Q\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\displaystyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\textstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.75}{$\scriptscriptstyle\triangleright$}}}}}A)\right\}.

In string diagrams, we shall write an element in
\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P)
as

\hskip 2.45836ptg\hskip 2.45836pt
P
Q

Lemma 5.1
.

The definition of
\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P)
is independent of the choice of epimorphism
Q\xrightarrow{q}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{1}
.

Proof.

Let
Q\xrightarrow{q}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{1}
and
Q^{\prime}\xrightarrow{q^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mkern-14.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{1}
be two choices of epimorphism from a projective to
\mathbb{1}
, and let
{\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P)}_{Q}
and
{\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P)}_{Q^{\prime}}
denote the resulting subspaces of
\mathcal{C}(P,A)
. We obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

Then

{\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P)}_{Q}=\{\,(q\otimes A)\circ f^{1}\mid f\in\mathfrak{J}(P,Q)\,\}=\{\,(q^{\prime}\otimes A)\circ(h\otimes A)\circ f^{1}\mid f\in\mathfrak{J}(P,Q)\,\}\subseteq{\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P)}_{Q^{\prime}},

and similarly
{\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P)}_{Q^{\prime}}\subseteq{\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P)}_{Q}
.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

The collection
\{\,\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P)\mid P\in\mathcal{C}_{p}\,\}

\,{\mathcal{C}({-},A)}_{|\mathcal{C}_{p}}

5.2.
From mixed subfunctors to
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideals

Definition 5.2
.

Let
\mathtt{I}
be a mixed subfunctor of
{\mathcal{C}({-},A)}_{|\mathcal{C}_{p}}

(5.2)

\mathbb{R}(\mathtt{I})(P,Q)=\left\{{((Q\otimes f)\circ(\mathrm{coev}_{{Q}}\otimes P))}^{A}\;|\;f\in\mathtt{I}({\vphantom{\!Q}}^{\vee}\!Q\otimes P)\right\}.

.

The assignment of
Equation 5.2
defines a
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal
\mathbb{R}(\mathtt{I})
in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}

.
In the following equation, we write
\mathbb{R}(f)\circ g
on the left-hand side, and on the right-hand side we have the morphism

\hskip 5.0ptg\hskip 5.0pt

=

\hskip 1.00006ptf\hskip 1.00006pt

\hskip 5.0ptg\hskip 5.0pt

5.3.
The bijection

Proposition 5.4
.

For
\mathfrak{J}
a
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal in
\operatorname{Kl}_{\mathcal{C}_{p}\otimes A}
, we have
\mathbb{RS}(\mathfrak{J})=\mathfrak{J}
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})(P,Q)=\left\{\scalebox{0.8}{\,\hbox to33.28pt{\vbox to47.24pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 18.28297pt\lower-21.83957pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \hbox to0.0pt{
{

Since
\mathfrak{J}
is a
\mathcal{C}_{p}
-stable ideal, one obtains that

\in\mathfrak{J}
\implies
\in\mathfrak{J}
,
and hence
\mathbb{R}\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})\subseteq\mathfrak{J}
.

The inclusion
\mathfrak{J}\subseteq\mathbb{R}\mathbb{S}(\mathfrak{J})
follows from the fact that

=

∎

Proposition 5.5
.

For
\mathtt{I}
a representable ideal in
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
, we have
\mathbb{SR}(\mathtt{I})=\mathtt{I}
.

Proof.

One obtains that
\mathbb{SR}(\mathtt{I})\subseteq\mathtt{I}
by the following calculation,
together with the fact that

\hskip 2.5ptg\hskip 2.5pt
\hskip 2.5ptg\hskip 2.5pt
\in\mathtt{I}\implies
\in\mathtt{I}

,
as
\mathtt{I}
is a subpresheaf of
\mathcal{C}({-},A)
